      - **Outline action:** 需检索该综述引用的原始研究文献（如文中提及的 Zhang et al. ref 52, Wang et al. ref 53, 及 ref 24），查看其是否包含人体样本的肌苷定量数据；同时应专门搜索涉及人体肿瘤组织代谢组学与 ICI 疗效关联的临床研究，以获取肌苷在人体 TME 中的具体浓度阈值及其与临床结局的统计相关性证据。
      - **Outline action recovered by policy:** False
      - **Remaining gap:** 该综述未提供人体肿瘤微环境（TME）内肌苷的绝对生理浓度数值（如μM 或 ng/mL），也未报告人体临床队列中肌苷水平与 ICI 疗效（如 OS、PFS、ORR）的具体统计学关联数据。文中关于肌苷浓度的描述主要引用了小鼠模型的血浆代谢组分析或体外实验背景，缺乏针对接受 ICI 治疗的人类患者肿瘤组织或间质液的实测浓度数据。
      - **Remaining gap recovered by policy:** False
      - **Status:** unmet
###### Useful material
      - 该综述详细阐述了肌苷（inosine）作为肠道微生物代谢物调节抗肿瘤免疫的三大分子机制：(1) 特异性抑制肿瘤细胞内的泛素样修饰激活酶 6（UBA6）活性，提高肿瘤免疫原性；(2) 结合 T 淋巴细胞上的腺苷 A2A 受体，激活 cAMP-CREB 信号级联，促进 Th1 细胞分化及肿瘤浸润；(3) 在葡萄糖受限的肿瘤微环境（TME）中作为替代能源底物维持效应 T 细胞的生存和细胞毒性功能。
      - 文中指出肌苷由特定肠道菌群（如假长双歧杆菌 Bifidobacterium pseudolongum、约氏乳杆菌 Lactobacillus johnsonii 等）合成，并在免疫检查点阻断（ICB）治疗破坏肠道屏障完整性后易位至体循环，从而激活抗肿瘤 T 细胞。
      - 综述提及了临床转化潜力，指出在存在肌苷代谢缺陷的实体瘤中，外源性肌苷补充可能恢复免疫细胞代谢适应性，从而增强 ICI 疗效，这为理解肌苷浓度与疗效的关系提供了理论框架。

### The gut microbiome in lung cancer: from pathogenesis to precision therapy

- Source handle: `P0297`
- Paper ID: `8211fc48bb00f738157c8859ad0748cc7d220f09`
- DOI: 10.3389/fmicb.2025.1606684

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** The authors conducted a narrative synthesis of literature from microbiology, immunology, and oncology. They organized the content into thematic sections: introduction to the gut-lung axis, alterations in microbiota composition and metabolism in lung cancer patients, microbial biomarkers for early detection, prediction of immunotherapy response, modulation of drug efficacy, microbiota-targeted therapeutic interventions, and mechanistic insights. The review integrates findings from clinical studies, animal models, and multi-omics analyses to construct a cohesive understanding of the gut-lung axis in lung cancer.
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** Provides a comprehensive overview of the gut-lung axis in lung cancer, linking specific microbial taxa and metabolites to clinical outcomes in diagnosis and immunotherapy.
      - **边界与限制:** As a narrative review, it does not present new primary data or perform a systematic meta-analysis. The causal relationships described are largely inferred from correlative clinical studies and preclinical models, which may vary across populations.
    - **2.**
      - **Contribution:** Identifies specific microbial signatures (e.g., A. muciniphila, SCFA producers) and metabolic profiles as potential biomarkers for predicting ICI response.
      - **边界与限制:** The generalizability of these biomarkers depends on the heterogeneity of patient cohorts and sequencing methodologies used in the cited studies, which are not standardized in this review.
    - **3.**
      - **Contribution:** Discusses therapeutic interventions like FMT and probiotics as adjuncts to cancer therapy, highlighting their potential to overcome resistance.
      - **边界与限制:** Clinical translation faces challenges including interindividual variability, lack of standardized protocols for FMT/probiotics, and long-term safety concerns, which are noted but not resolved.
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** In lung cancer patients compared to healthy controls
      - **研究发现:** Patients exhibit dysbiosis characterized by increased abundance of Ruminococcus, Enterobacteriaceae, Lachnospiraceae, and decreased levels of Faecalibacterium, Bifidobacterium, and Veillonella. Metabolic profiles show reduced SCFA production and increased lactate.
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** For early detection of lung cancer
      - **研究发现:** A panel of 13 microbial biomarkers was identified with high diagnostic potential, achieving an AUC of 92.4% in discovery and 67.7% in validation cohorts using a Patient Discrimination Index (PDI).
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** Predicting response to Immune Checkpoint Inhibitors (ICIs)
      - **研究发现:** Baseline presence of Akkermansia muciniphila, Faecalibacterium spp., and Clostridiales correlates with improved ICI outcomes. Higher levels of acetate, propionate, and butyrate are associated with prolonged progression-free survival. A TOPOSCORE integrating species interaction groups and A. muciniphila abundance predicts individual responses.
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** Impact of antibiotics and probiotics on ICI efficacy
      - **研究发现:** Antibiotic use prior to ICI therapy is associated with reduced overall survival and impaired efficacy. Probiotic supplementation (e.g., Bifidobacterium, Clostridium butyricum) may enhance ICI efficacy and improve survival outcomes in NSCLC patients.
    - **5.**
      - **适用条件:** Mechanisms of action
      - **研究发现:** Gut microbes modulate lung immunity via metabolites (SCFAs), antigen presentation, and immune cell trafficking. Specific bacteria like A. muciniphila can translocate to tumor tissues, restructure the intratumoral ecosystem, and restore exhausted CD8+ T cells, enhancing anti-PD-1 therapy efficacy.
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** To synthesize existing evidence regarding how the gut microbiome influences lung cancer pathogenesis and treatment efficacy, particularly in the context of immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs), and to identify potential therapeutic strategies and biomarkers derived from this interaction.
    - **Research scope:** The review focuses on the gut-lung axis in the context of lung cancer, specifically examining non-small cell lung carcinoma (NSCLC) and small cell lung carcinoma. It covers pathogenesis, early detection biomarkers, mechanisms of immune modulation, and therapeutic interventions including immunotherapy response prediction and microbiota-targeted treatments.
    - **Work summary:** This is a narrative review synthesizing current knowledge on the role of the gut microbiome in lung cancer development, diagnosis, and treatment. It outlines the bidirectional communication between intestinal microbiota and pulmonary health via immune, neural, and metabolic pathways. The paper highlights microbial signatures predictive of disease and therapy response, discusses microbiota-targeted interventions such as probiotics and fecal microbiota transplantation (FMT), and elucidates mechanistic insights into microbial-immune crosstalk. It aims to provide a comprehensive framework for leveraging the gut microbiome in personalized lung cancer management.

#### Review planning account from b

    - **1.**

      - **本节作用:** Background context on the gut-lung axis concept
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** Provides practical examples of how microbiome modulation is being explored clinically, useful for discussing future directions or intervention strategies.
      - **Material:** Discussion of probiotics, prebiotics, FMT, and dietary interventions as adjuncts to immunotherapy.
      - **本节作用:** Examples of therapeutic interventions
    - **3.**
      - **Connection to review:** Allows for comparison of microbiome-Ici interactions across different solid tumors, though the primary focus remains lung cancer.
      - **Material:** Brief mentions of melanoma, renal, and bladder carcinomas regarding antibiotic impact on ICIs.
      - **本节作用:** Contrast with other cancer types
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** Focuses primarily on lung cancer; while it mentions other cancers briefly, the detailed evidence is lung-specific. Causal inference is limited to observational associations and preclinical models.
      - **Contribution:** Details the correlation between specific gut microbiota compositions (e.g., A. muciniphila, SCFA-producing bacteria) and improved clinical outcomes in solid tumors treated with ICIs. It also notes that antibiotic use negatively impacts ICI efficacy.
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** Can be used to support arguments about the positive or negative influence of specific microbial taxa on ICI efficacy in solid tumors. Provides examples of predictive biomarkers.

It provides detailed descriptions of microbial signatures associated with ICI response, mechanistic pathways involving metabolites and immune cells, and emerging therapeutic strategies. It is highly relevant for facets concerning how the microbiome modulates ICI efficacy and the underlying mechanisms.
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - The review is a narrative synthesis, not a systematic review or meta-analysis; therefore, it does not provide pooled effect sizes or rigorous statistical aggregation of all available evidence.
    - Many mechanistic claims are based on preclinical (mouse) models or correlative clinical studies; causal relationships in humans are not definitively proven within this text.
    - The term 'solid tumours' in the user's plan is broader than the paper's primary focus on lung cancer; users must extrapolate carefully when applying lung-specific findings to other solid tumors.

### Microbiome Modulation for the Treatment of Solid Neoplasms

- Source handle: `P0215`
- Paper ID: `5b4da7a4eb5afd77f649e4aaa0b69c1db2666e97`
- DOI: 10.1200/jco-25-00374

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** As a review article, the approach involves a critical appraisal of published literature rather than primary data generation. The authors systematically discuss: 1) Preclinical models identifying key bacterial species; 2) Clinical correlations between stool microbiome composition and ICI outcomes; 3) The impact of antibiotics on survival; 4) Specific intervention trials for prebiotics (dietary fiber), probiotics (CBM588, SER-401), and FMT across different tumor types and lines of therapy. They compare results against historical benchmarks and note limitations such as small sample sizes and heterogeneity in trial designs.
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** Provides a comprehensive synthesis of current evidence linking gut microbiome composition to ICI efficacy in solid tumors, categorizing strategies into prebiotics, probiotics, and FMT.
      - **边界与限制:** The field lacks large, definitive randomized trials; most evidence comes from small phase I/II studies or retrospective analyses. The review acknowledges that widespread adoption is premature and mechanisms are not fully elucidated.
    - **2.**
      - **Contribution:** Highlights the complexity of microbial effects, noting that both low and excessively high levels of certain bacteria (e.g., Akkermansia) may be suboptimal, suggesting a need for balanced composition rather than single-species enrichment.
      - **边界与限制:** Does not provide causal mechanistic proof for all observed associations; many findings are correlative.
    - **3.**
      - **Contribution:** Identifies specific ongoing trials and future directions, such as stratification based on baseline microbiome composition.
      - **边界与限制:** Cannot predict the success of future trials or resolve uncertainties regarding universal vs. personalized application of microbiome therapies.
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** Preclinical and observational clinical studies
      - **研究发现:** Specific bacterial taxa (Ruminococcaceae, Akkermansia, Bifidobacterium) correlate with favorable responses to anti-PD-1 antibodies, while antibiotic use before/during ICI initiation is associated with higher primary resistance and shorter overall survival.
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** Clinical trials of Probiotics (CBM588)
      - **研究发现:** Small prospective trials in mRCC showed significantly improved progression-free survival and overall response rates when CBM588 was added to ICI-based regimens, though sample sizes were limited (n=30 per arm).
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** Clinical trials of FMT
      - **研究发现:** FMT combined with ICIs showed encouraging results in refractory melanoma (ORR 30%) and frontline settings for melanoma (ORR 65%), NSCLC (ORR 77%), and RCC (12-month PFS improved from 35% to 67%). However, benefits were not consistent in all contexts, such as microsatellite-stable colorectal cancer where outcomes were comparable to historical controls without FMT.
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** Biomarker development
      - **研究发现:** The TOPOSCORE biomarker, integrating Akkermansia with other microbial groups, showed significant correlation with overall survival in cohorts of mNSCLC, mRCC, and urothelial carcinoma.
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** The paper addresses the growing interest in microbiome modulation as a therapeutic strategy to overcome resistance to ICIs in oncology. It seeks to summarize current evidence regarding which microbial species are beneficial or detrimental, how disruption affects outcomes, and whether specific modulation strategies (prebiotics, probiotics, FMT) show clinical benefit.
    - **Research scope:** The review focuses on the interplay between the gut microbiome and immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs) in patients with solid malignancies, specifically advanced melanoma, metastatic renal cell carcinoma (mRCC), and metastatic non–small cell lung cancer (mNSCLC). It covers preclinical evidence, clinical observations of microbial associations, and various modulation strategies including prebiotics, probiotics, and fecal microbiota transplantation (FMT).
    - **Work summary:** This article is a narrative review that critically appraises existing studies on how gut microbiome modulation can enhance the efficacy of ICIs in solid tumors. It synthesizes preclinical findings linking specific bacterial taxa to treatment response, discusses the negative impact of antibiotic-induced dysbiosis, and evaluates clinical data from trials involving dietary interventions, probiotic supplementation, and FMT. The authors highlight promising signals but emphasize the lack of large-scale randomized controlled trials needed to establish standard of care.

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**
      - **Connection to review:** Useful for introducing the topic and justifying the focus on microbiome in ICI resistance/sensitivity.
      - **Material:** The review establishes the rationale for microbiome modulation, citing preclinical discoveries (Sivan et al., Vétizou et al.) and the general interplay between gut microbiome and immunity.
      - **本节作用:** Background and Context
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** Illustrates that microbiome effects are context-dependent (tumor type, prior treatments), supporting nuanced discussions in the review about patient selection and disease specificity.
      - **Material:** Contrasting results: FMT worked well in melanoma/RCC/NSCLC but showed no added benefit over historical controls in microsatellite-stable colorectal cancer.
      - **本节作用:** Example of Heterogeneity in Response
    - **3.**
      - **Connection to review:** Highlights limitations in current research design that the broader review should address when evaluating the strength of evidence.
      - **Material:** Discussion of small sample sizes, lack of standardization in FMT donors (responders vs. healthy volunteers), and conflicting data on single-biomarker utility (Akkermansia levels).
      - **本节作用:** Methodological Challenges
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** Most clinical data are from small, non-randomized, or early-phase trials. Cannot definitively prove causality for all associations. The review itself notes that large RCTs are lacking.
      - **Contribution:** Summarizes clinical evidence showing that specific gut microbes (Ruminococcaceae, Akkermansia, Bifidobacterium) are associated with improved ICI outcomes, while antibiotic disruption correlates with poorer survival. Provides ORR and PFS data from FMT and probiotic trials in melanoma, RCC, and NSCLC.
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** Can be used to answer how the microbiome modulates efficacy by providing observational correlations and early-phase clinical trial results. Supports the argument that microbiome status is a determinant of ICI success.
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** The review is descriptive and does not present new mechanistic experiments. Mechanisms are largely inferred from preclinical models or translational analyses of small cohorts. Does not provide detailed molecular pathway analysis.
      - **Contribution:** Discusses potential mechanisms indirectly through observed effects: e.g., dietary fiber enhancing postbiotic production, vancomycin preconditioning disrupting immunity, and intratumoral Gammaproteobacteria hindering gemcitabine response. Mentions the role of short-chain fatty acids.
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** Provides context for mechanistic hypotheses. Can be used to illustrate proposed pathways (e.g., SCFA production, immune activation) that require further investigation.
    - **Planning summary:** This review serves as a foundational overview for understanding the current state of microbiome modulation in ICI-treated solid tumors. It provides concrete examples of successful and failed interventions, specific bacterial markers associated with response, and the methodological challenges in this field. It is particularly useful for establishing the background of why microbiome matters, summarizing the landscape of clinical trials, and highlighting the gap between preclinical promise and clinical validation.
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - The review is a narrative summary, not a systematic meta-analysis. Do not treat its conclusions as definitive consensus.
    - Clinical trial results cited are primarily from Phase I/II studies with small sample sizes; avoid overstating efficacy as established fact.
    - Mechanistic claims are largely based on preclinical models or correlational clinical data; distinguish between observed association and proven mechanism.
    - The review explicitly states that large randomized trials are lacking; do not imply that these strategies are standard of care.

### Navigating the gut–metabolite–immune axis: enhancing efficacy and mitigating toxicity of immune checkpoint inhibitors

- Source handle: `P0333`
- Paper ID: `8e25ec1d06aea0a73a479436c268bc6fab267259`
- DOI: 10.3389/fimmu.2026.1803970

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** The authors conducted a comprehensive literature search across PubMed/MEDLINE, Web of Science Core Collection, and Embase up to May 1, 2026. They included original studies, RCTs, cohort studies, and preclinical investigations examining the effects of gut microbiota or metabolites on ICI efficacy or toxicities. Data extraction was guided by STORMS guidelines. As a narrative review, formal risk-of-bias assessment, quality grading, and statistical meta-analysis were not performed. The synthesis is organized thematically: first covering ICI mechanisms, then the heterogeneity of microbiome-ICI interactions, followed by specific metabolite classes, clinical heterogeneity of irAEs, and finally microbiome-based interventions.
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** Provides a comprehensive mechanistic synthesis linking specific gut microbes and metabolites to ICI efficacy and irAE mitigation, categorizing interventions by their molecular targets (STING, NOD2, AhR, etc.).
      - **边界与限制:** As a narrative review, it lacks formal risk-of-bias assessment and statistical meta-analysis. Findings are largely derived from preclinical models or small clinical cohorts with limited covariate adjustment. The review acknowledges significant methodological heterogeneity across studies and notes that many proposed biomarkers and interventions require validation in large-scale, standardized longitudinal studies.
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** Melanoma patients treated with anti-CTLA-4 (ipilimumab)
      - **研究发现:** CTLA-4 blockade reshapes the microbiota, favoring Bacteroides species (e.g., B. fragilis, B. thetaiotaomicron), which are critical for antitumor efficacy.
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** NSCLC patients treated with anti-PD-1
      - **研究发现:** High relative abundance of Akkermansia muciniphila (Akk-high) was associated with worse overall survival compared to Akk-low groups, challenging the binary 'good vs bad' bacteria concept and highlighting cohort/cancer-type dependency.
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** Various ICI regimens
      - **研究发现:** Microbial influence is context-dependent; different ICIs rely on distinct microbial signatures (e.g., Faecalibacterium/Ruminococcaceae for anti-PD-1 vs. Bacteroides for anti-CTLA-4).
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** Tryptophan metabolites (3-IAld, IPA)
      - **研究发现:** 3-IAld alleviates colitis and enhances efficacy via AhR/IL-22; IPA provides cardioprotection via AhR/PI3K/AKT signaling.
    - **5.**
      - **适用条件:** SCFAs (Butyrate, Propionate, Acetate)
      - **研究发现:** Butyrate protects against cardiotoxicity via PPARa-CYP4X1 and enhances antigen presentation via HDAC inhibition; Propionate/Acetate promote Treg homeostasis and memory CD8+ T-cell differentiation.
    - **6.**
      - **适用条件:** TMAO
      - **研究发现:** Triggers immunogenic tumor cell death (pyroptosis) via PERK-GSDME pathway, recruiting CD8+ T cells.
    - **7.**
      - **适用条件:** Probiotics (LGG, BF839, Akk)
      - **研究发现:** LGG and BF839 activate cGAS-STING axis in dendritic cells to enhance CD8+ T cell infiltration; Akk produces c-di-AMP which directly activates STING.
    - **8.**
      - **适用条件:** FMT interventions
      - **研究发现:** FMT from long-term responders to PD-1 therapy showed higher efficacy than healthy donors; response correlated with loss of deleterious baseline species rather than just engraftment of donor strains.
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** The clinical utility of ICIs is limited by interpatient variability in efficacy and the occurrence of systemic toxicities (irAEs). The review aims to systematically delineate the microbiome-metabolite-immune axis to understand these variations and optimize the balance between therapeutic gain and toxicity, ultimately guiding the development of precise microbial interventions.
    - **Research scope:** The review focuses on the gut microbiome and its metabolic byproducts as systemic regulators of immune function, specifically in the context of cancer immunotherapy using immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs). It covers mechanisms of ICI action (PD-1/PD-L1, CTLA-4, LAG-3, TIGIT, TIM-3), the heterogeneity of microbiome-ICI interactions across different cancer types and cohorts, microbial metabolites (tryptophan derivatives, SCFAs, TMAO, inosine, bile acids, polyamines) as mediators of efficacy and toxicity, and microbiome-based interventions including probiotics, FMT, postbiotics, and dietary fiber.
    - **Work summary:** This narrative review synthesizes current evidence on how the gut microbiota and microbe-derived metabolites regulate the efficacy and immune-related adverse events (irAEs) of ICIs. It highlights specific taxa like Bacteroides, Bifidobacterium, and Akkermansia muciniphila that potentiate therapeutic responses via pathways such as cGAS-STING and NOD2. The review also details how microbial metabolites protect organs like the heart and colon from inflammation while enhancing anti-tumor immunity. It evaluates emerging interventions like FMT and next-generation probiotics, noting that while preclinical and Phase I trials show feasibility, substantial hurdles remain regarding cohort heterogeneity, methodological standardization, and the need for large-scale longitudinal studies to establish reproducible predictive models.

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**
      - **Connection to review:** Discusses the limitations of current microbiome research, including methodological heterogeneity, lack of standardized protocols, and the gap between preclinical findings and clinical translation.
      - **Material:** Critiques the reliance on single-taxa abundance, advocates for topology-based approaches, and highlights logistical barriers like metagenomic turnaround times for precision nutrition.
      - **本节作用:** Provides background on the challenges and future directions in the field, useful for discussing the current state of evidence and areas needing further research.
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** Summarizes various microbiome-based interventions, including FMT, probiotics, postbiotics, and dietary fiber, detailing their potential benefits and risks.
      - **Material:** Describes FMT from responders vs. healthy donors, encapsulation technologies for probiotics, and the double-edged nature of antibiotic preconditioning and high-fiber diets.
      - **本节作用:** Offers examples of clinical interventions and their outcomes, useful for comparing different strategies to modulate the microbiome for improved ICI outcomes.
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** Findings are based on a synthesis of various studies with differing methodologies; causal relationships are often inferred from preclinical models or observational data rather than definitive clinical trials.
      - **Contribution:** Details the context-dependent nature of microbiome-ICI interactions, showing that beneficial taxa vary by cancer type and ICI regimen (e.g., Bacteroides for CTLA-4, Faecalibacterium for PD-1, variable role of Akk). It also discusses the trade-off between efficacy and toxicity, noting that some microbes protective in one context may be detrimental in another.
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** Can be used to explain why microbiome signatures are not universal and depend on specific treatment protocols and patient populations. Helps address questions about heterogeneity in ICI response.
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** Many mechanisms are described from murine models or in vitro studies; human concentration thresholds and spatial dynamics within the tumor microenvironment are noted as undefined.
      - **Contribution:** Provides specific molecular mechanisms linking microbes/metabolites to immune modulation. For example, it describes how LGG and BF839 activate the cGAS-STING pathway, how tryptophan metabolites act via AhR, and how SCFAs influence T-cell function and barrier integrity through epigenetic and receptor-mediated pathways.
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** Offers detailed mechanistic explanations for how gut microbes influence ICI efficacy and toxicity, suitable for sections discussing biological pathways and molecular targets.
    - **Planning summary:** This review offers a structured framework for understanding the 'gut-metabolite-immune' axis in the context of solid tumor immunotherapy. It provides detailed mechanistic insights into how specific microbial taxa and metabolites modulate ICI efficacy and toxicity, serving as a valuable resource for explaining biological mechanisms and contextualizing clinical heterogeneity. It also summarizes the current state of microbiome-based interventions, highlighting both promising strategies and translational barriers.
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - The review is a narrative synthesis and does not provide quantitative meta-analytic results; effect sizes and statistical significance should not be inferred from this text alone.
    - Many mechanistic insights are derived from preclinical models (mice) or small clinical cohorts; extrapolation to broader human populations requires caution.
    - The role of specific microbes (e.g., Akkermansia) can be context-dependent and even contradictory across studies; avoid treating any single taxon as universally beneficial or detrimental.
    - Claims about the efficacy of interventions like FMT or probiotics are primarily based on early-phase trials or preclinical data; robust clinical validation is still needed.
##### Topic handles
    - Immune Checkpoint Inhibitors (ICIs): PD-1, PD-L1, CTLA-4, LAG-3, TIGIT, TIM-3
    - Gut Microbiota Taxa: Bacteroides, Bifidobacterium, Akkermansia muciniphila, Faecalibacterium, Ruminococcaceae
    - Microbial Metabolites: Short-chain fatty acids (SCFAs), Tryptophan derivatives (3-IAld, IPA, ILA), TMAO, Inosine, Secondary bile acids, Polyamines
    - Signaling Pathways: cGAS-STING, NOD2, AhR, PPARa, HDAC, PERK-GSDME
    - Interventions: Fecal Microbiota Transplantation (FMT), Probiotics (LGG, BF839), Postbiotics, Dietary Fiber
    - Clinical Outcomes: Objective Response Rate (ORR), Progression-Free Survival (PFS), Immune-related Adverse Events (irAEs)

### Integrative Oncology Approaches to Supporting Immune Checkpoint Inhibitor Treatment of Solid Tumours

- Source handle: `P0343`
- Paper ID: `93c5dd39ff702a807d9b564fd630d241e76ebcba`
- DOI: 10.1007/s11912-023-01492-4

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** 作为一篇叙述性综述，作者基于现有文献（包括观察性研究、临床试验、指南和机制研究），系统性地归纳了影响ICI疗效的非肿瘤内在因素（如炎症、微生物群、生活方式），并据此分类讨论了各类整合肿瘤学干预手段（营养、运动、微生物调节、补充剂、心理干预、草药等）的证据等级、潜在机制及临床建议。
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** 提供了整合肿瘤学策略支持ICI治疗的全面概览，特别强调了从“预测”转向“响应行动”的转化医学思路（如利用炎症标志物指导干预时机）。
      - **边界与限制:** 所引用的多数证据来自观察性研究或小样本试验，缺乏大规模随机对照试验（RCT）的确证；对于许多干预措施（如特定益生菌、中草药、身心疗法），其因果效应和最佳剂量/方案尚未明确。
    - **2.**
      - **Contribution:** 明确了医疗大麻在ICI治疗中的潜在负面信号，以及维生素D补充的具体获益阈值。
      - **边界与限制:** 对某些新兴领域（如真菌疗法、具体中药方剂）仅提及预临床证据或正在进行的试验，缺乏直接的临床结果支持。
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** 成人实体瘤患者，基线或治疗期间
      - **研究发现:** 系统性炎症标志物（如NLR, CRP, LIPI）升高与较差的ICI预后相关；CRP flare（治疗后初期CRP升高后下降）可能与良好的免疫反应相关。
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** 饮食干预
      - **研究发现:** 摄入至少20g/天膳食纤维的地中海式饮食与晚期黑色素瘤患者的无进展生存期（PFS）延长相关；避免超加工食品和高糖饮料有助于维持健康的肠道微生物群。
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** 运动干预
      - **研究发现:** 回顾性研究显示，接受仑伐替尼联合抗PD-1治疗的肝细胞癌患者中，活跃运动组比久坐组具有更长的总生存期（OS）和无进展生存期（PFS）；但针对ICI的特异性RCT数据仍有限。
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** 肠道微生物群调节
      - **研究发现:** 粪菌移植（FMT）可使部分ICI耐药患者产生客观缓解；口服活体生物疗法（LBTs，如MRx0518, CBM588）显示出免疫调节和增强ICI疗效的潜力，且可能减轻胃肠道irAEs。益生菌效果存在争议，取决于菌株特异性。
    - **5.**
      - **适用条件:** 维生素D补充
      - **研究发现:** PROVIDENCE前瞻性研究表明，将血清25-羟基维生素D水平补充至≥75 nmol/l可显著延长晚期癌症患者的OS，降低疾病控制失败风险，并减少甲状腺irAEs的发生率。
    - **6.**
      - **适用条件:** 医疗大麻使用
      - **研究发现:** 一项前瞻性观察性研究发现，医疗大麻的使用与时间至肿瘤进展缩短及总生存期（OS）显著降低相关；尽管另一项小样本研究未达统计学显著差异，但鉴于潜在风险，建议谨慎使用。
    - **7.**
      - **适用条件:** 慢性压力与身心疗法
      - **研究发现:** 治疗前的情绪困扰和慢性心理压力被证实是黑色素瘤和非小细胞肺癌（NSCLC）患者ICI疗效差的独立预后因素；身心疗法（瑜伽、太极等）在此领域尚需进一步临床研究。
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** 随着ICI在实体瘤中的广泛应用，如何识别并利用低成本、低毒性的整合肿瘤学策略来支持ICI治疗，以提高疗效、改善预后并最小化免疫相关不良事件（irAEs）。
    - **Research scope:** 成人实体瘤患者接受免疫检查点抑制剂（ICI）治疗期间，整合肿瘤学（Integrative Oncology, IO）策略的应用与潜力。涵盖营养、运动、微生物组调节、补充剂及心理干预等支持性措施。
    - **Work summary:** 本文是一篇综述，旨在评估整合肿瘤学策略在支持成人实体瘤患者进行免疫检查点抑制剂（ICI）治疗中的作用和实际应用。文章指出，除了肿瘤内在因素外，宿主相关的系统性炎症、肠道微生物群、饮食和遗传背景显著影响ICI疗效。综述梳理了当前有前景的支持性干预措施，包括地中海式高纤维饮食、规律运动、活体生物疗法、减少质子泵抑制剂（PPI）和抗生素使用、维生素D补充等，并警告医疗大麻可能带来的生存期缩短风险。文章强调通过多模式术前康复（prehabilitation）和术中支持来优化患者状态，以改善治疗效果并最小化不良反应。

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**
      - **Connection to review:** 帮助界定综述的范围，说明除了微生物群外，还有哪些宿主因素（如炎症、压力、营养）共同作用影响ICI疗效。
      - **Material:** 综述定义了整合肿瘤学，概述了术前康复（prehabilitation）的概念，以及除微生物群外的其他支持策略（运动、维生素D、大麻禁忌）。
      - **本节作用:** 提供背景信息：整合肿瘤学在ICI治疗中的整体定位和现状
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** 通过对比积极干预（如LBTs、高纤维饮食）与潜在有害干预（如医疗大麻、滥用抗生素）的效果，强化微生物群/炎症调节的重要性。
      - **Material:** 医疗大麻导致OS缩短的观察性数据；维生素D补充降低irAEs的数据；VAC（槲寄生提取物）的安全性数据。
      - **本节作用:** 提供对比案例：不同干预措施的效果差异和安全警示
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** 这些发现主要基于观察性研究和小型试点研究，因果关系的确立程度不一；不同癌症类型（主要是黑色素瘤、NSCLC、HCC）的结果不能直接泛化到所有实体瘤。
      - **Contribution:** 提供了肠道微生物群调节ICI疗效的临床证据链：包括FMT诱导缓解的案例、LBTs（如MRx0518, CBM588）增强疗效和减轻irAEs的数据、以及饮食纤维（>20g/d）与PFS延长的关联。同时指出了益生菌效果的菌株特异性和矛盾性。
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** 用于描述肠道微生物群作为ICI疗效关键调节因子的临床表现；列举具体的微生物干预策略及其对肿瘤反应（ORR, PFS, OS）的影响方向。
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** 机制部分多为动物模型或体外研究推论（如L. reuteri的机制），临床人体内的确切分子通路细节在所给材料中未完全展开；压力机制主要基于动物研究和人类相关性观察，直接因果链条需结合其他文献验证。
      - **Contribution:** 阐述了微生物群影响ICI疗效的部分机制路径：包括短链脂肪酸（SCFAs）驱动CD4/CD8 T细胞激活、促进IFN表达；特定菌株代谢物（如I3A）促进肿瘤内CD8+ T细胞浸润；以及系统性炎症（IL-6, IL-8）作为负向调节因子。同时提到了慢性压力通过HPA轴/SNS改变肿瘤微环境（TME）的间接机制。
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** 用于解释微生物群介导ICI疗效的生物学机制，特别是免疫细胞活化、细胞因子释放和肿瘤微环境重塑方面的通路。
    - **Planning summary:** 该综述为理解肠道微生物群如何调节ICI疗效提供了重要的临床背景和机制线索。它汇总了关于微生物群组成、饮食纤维、活体生物疗法（LBTs）、粪菌移植（FMT）以及抗生素/PPI使用对ICI反应影响的证据。虽然它本身不提出新的分子机制实验数据，但它清晰地指出了微生物群作为关键中介变量的地位，并列出了具体的干预手段（如特定菌株、纤维摄入量）及其对应的临床结局变化，可直接用于回答“微生物群如何调节ICI疗效”及其背后的临床关联。
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - 该综述主要聚焦于成人实体瘤，特别是黑色素瘤、NSCLC、肾细胞癌和肝癌，结果可能不适用于血液肿瘤或其他罕见实体瘤。
    - 关于肠道微生物群的许多结论来自观察性研究或小样本试验，需谨慎推断因果关系；例如，饮食纤维的益处可能与整体健康生活方式有关，而非单一成分作用。
    - 益生菌的效果具有高度的菌株特异性，综述中提到的矛盾结果（Spencer vs Bender）提示不能一概而论地推荐或禁止所有益生菌。
    - 医疗大麻的负面结果主要来自观察性研究，可能存在混杂因素（如疼痛严重程度、吸烟史），尽管综述建议谨慎，但不能视为绝对的禁忌证证据。

### Gut Microbiota in Advanced Non-small-cell Lung Cancer Receiving Chemo-immunotherapy: An Ancillary Biomarker Study from the Phase III trial JCOG2007 (NIPPON).

- Source handle: `P0585`
- Paper ID: `8016df1aa5a8160b7ccd412fa6851a6a508e2731`
- DOI: 10.1016/j.jtho.2025.02.026

  - **Available material depth:** abstract

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** 这是一项嵌入在随机对照III期临床试验（JCOG2007, NIPPON）中的辅助生物标志物研究。研究设计包括：1. 队列选择：从入组的295名患者中筛选出270名提供基线粪便样本的患者进行分析。2. 分组比较：比较两种一线治疗方案（PC vs NIC）。3. 微生物分析：对基线粪便样本进行16S rDNA测序，进行α/β多样性分析和LEfSe差异丰度分析。4. 临床关联分析：将微生物特征（多样性、特定菌属丰度）与主要临床终点（OS超过12或18个月的亚群区分、总体OS、≥Grade 4严重不良事件发生率）进行统计学关联分析，并进行了按治疗方案的亚组分析。
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** 提供了在大型III期临床试验背景下，肠道微生物群与NSCLC化疗-免疫治疗预后及安全性关联的具体实证数据。识别了特定的细菌属（如Fusicatenibacter, Butyricicoccus）作为潜在的预后和安全性标志物，并提示了方案特异性的微生物效应。
      - **边界与限制:** 仅基于摘要信息，无法评估多变量校正模型的完整性、多重检验校正方法、16S测序的具体流程细节及批次效应处理。结果主要关联观察性，因果机制未阐明。NIC组中Prevotellaceae NK3B31的结果需结合具体统计显著性和置信区间解读。
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** 整体队列（n=270），根据OS是否超过12或18个月划分的亚群
      - **研究发现:** β多样性分析显示不同生存时间亚群具有不同的微生物结构。LEfSe分析发现，Fusicatenibacter、Butyricicoccus和Blautia在长生存患者中富集。
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** 整体队列，关注严重不良事件（≥ Grade 4 AEs）
      - **研究发现:** 较低的微生物α多样性及某些特定菌属的存在与较高的严重AE风险相关。相反，有益菌属（如Fusicatenibacter和Butyricicoccus）的高丰度与较低的严重AE风险相关。
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** 按治疗方案分层（NIC臂 vs CP/PC臂）
      - **研究发现:** 在NIC组中，Fusicatenibacter和Butyricicoccus的高丰度与更好的OS相关（HR分别为0.56和0.52），优于CP组。而在NIC组中，Prevotellaceae NK3B31的高丰度与更高的死亡风险相关（HR=2.33）。
    - **Paper kind:** empirical
    - **Problem or question:** 尽管免疫治疗改变了NSCLC的治疗格局，但缺乏可靠的生物标志物来指导治疗选择。肠道微生物群虽被视为潜在生物标志物，但其在NSCLC化疗-免疫联合治疗中的具体作用尚不清楚。本研究试图回答：肠道微生物群的组成和多样性是否能预测晚期NSCLC患者在接受不同化疗-免疫联合方案时的预后（OS）和治疗安全性（严重AEs）？
    - **Research scope:** 针对无驱动基因改变的晚期非小细胞肺癌（NSCLC）患者，基于JCOG2007（NIPPON）III期临床试验的辅助生物标志物研究。研究对象为接受培美曲塞+铂类化疗联合帕博利珠单抗（PC组）或纳武利尤单抗+伊匹木单抗联合铂类化疗（NIC组）的患者，分析基线粪便样本中的肠道微生物群。
    - **Work summary:** 本研究旨在探索肠道微生物群作为晚期NSCLC化疗-免疫治疗疗效和安全性生物标志物的潜力。研究利用JCOG2007试验中270名有基线粪便样本患者的16S rDNA测序数据，分析了微生物多样性及特定菌属丰度与总生存期（OS）及严重不良事件（AEs）之间的关联。结果显示，微生物结构随生存时间延长而变化，特定产丁酸菌属富集于长生存患者，且与较低的严重AE风险相关；此外，不同治疗方案下微生物对预后的影响存在差异。

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**

      - **Material:** 研究背景指出免疫治疗改变了NSCLC治疗格局，但缺乏可靠生物标志物，肠道微生物群被视为潜在标志物。
      - **本节作用:** 背景与概念框架
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** 用于展示微生物效应的异质性，避免过度简化“好菌/坏菌”的二元论。提示在整合微生物数据时需要考虑具体的菌群组成和可能的拮抗作用。
      - **Material:** NIC组中Prevotellaceae NK3B31高丰度与高死亡风险（HR=2.33）相关，而Fusicatenibacter等则有益。
      - **本节作用:** 实例与反例
    - **3.**
      - **Connection to review:** 反映领域内对于微生物指导临床决策的展望，可用于综述的讨论部分，探讨未来转化医学的方向和挑战。
      - **Material:** 结论建议将肠道微生物谱分析整合到临床实践中以优化一线治疗策略。
      - **本节作用:** 实践知识
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** 结果来自NSCLC特定人群，且为观察性关联。NIC组中Prevotellaceae NK3B31显示负面关联，提示微生物效应具有复杂性和情境依赖性。不能直接外推至所有实体瘤或所有ICI单药治疗。
      - **Contribution:** 提供了肠道微生物群与ICI疗效关联的临床队列证据。具体发现：Fusicatenibacter和Butyricicoccus的高丰度与更长的OS相关，特别是在NIC（纳武利尤单抗+伊匹木单抗）方案中效果显著（HR 0.56/0.52）。同时，这些菌属也与较低的严重AE风险相关。
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** 用于支持综述中关于“有益微生物促进ICI疗效”的论点。可用于举例说明特定菌属（产丁酸菌）在改善预后中的作用。可用于讨论微生物与免疫治疗毒性的关联（安全性维度）。
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** 本研究未提供直接的机制验证（如动物模型、体外实验或代谢产物测量）。关联不等于因果。方案特异性结果的生物学解释需谨慎，可能涉及药物代谢、免疫微环境改变等多重因素。
      - **Contribution:** 提供了微生物群落结构与临床结局（生存期和毒性）的关联数据，暗示了微生物可能通过调节宿主免疫反应（进而影响疗效和毒性）发挥作用，但未直接测定分子机制。指出了不同免疫检查点阻断策略（PD-1 vs CTLA-4+PD-1）下微生物效应的差异性（方案特异性）。
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** 用于综述中讨论“微生物效应的情境依赖性”或“药物-微生物相互作用”。虽然不直接揭示分子机制，但其发现的特定菌属（如产丁酸菌）可作为后续机制研究的候选靶点或生物标志物。可用于论证为何需要针对不同免疫方案定制微生物干预策略。
    - **Planning summary:** 该论文为综述提供了关于肠道微生物群如何调节实体瘤（特别是NSCLC）免疫检查点抑制剂（ICI）疗效及安全性的直接临床证据。其核心价值在于不仅关联了微生物与疗效（OS），还关联了与免疫治疗密切相关的毒性（严重AEs），并揭示了不同免疫检查点抑制剂组合（PD-1 vs PD-1+CTLA-4）下的微生物响应差异，有助于回答“哪些微生物有益/有害”以及“机制是否受药物类型影响”的问题。
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - 该研究为观察性附属研究，关联结果不能直接推断因果关系。
    - 结果基于16S rDNA测序，分辨率限于属水平，且未提供功能基因组或代谢组数据，机制解释受限。
    - NIC组中Prevotellaceae NK3B31的负面结果需警惕，可能与样本量、统计效力或特定亚群特征有关，不宜简单概括为该类菌普遍有害。
    - 方案特异性结果（NIC vs PC）的差异可能受药物相互作用、患者基线特征或其他混杂因素影响，需谨慎解读。
    - 摘要未提供详细的统计校正方法（如多重检验校正、协变量调整），影响结果的确信度评估。

#### Supplement material
    - **Auxiliary material:** The study highlights regimen-specific effects, noting that microbial associations with outcomes differed between the PD-1 inhibitor-based regimen (PC) and the dual checkpoint blockade regimen (NIC). It identifies specific bacterial genera (Fusicatenibacter, Butyricicoccus, Blautia) enriched in patients with longer OS (>12 or >18 months). It also notes that lower microbial alpha diversity and presence of certain taxa were linked to a higher risk of serious (≥ Grade 4) AEs. The authors conclude that gut microbiota may serve as a biomarker for chemo-immunotherapy in advanced NSCLC.

    - **Useful material:** This study reports on the JCOG2007 trial, a completed phase III randomized controlled trial comparing pembrolizumab plus platinum doublet chemotherapy (PC) and nivolumab-ipilimumab plus platinum doublet chemotherapy (NIC) in treatment-naïve advanced non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) patients. The trial enrolled 295 patients, with an ancillary biomarker analysis performed on baseline fecal samples from 270 patients using 16S rDNA sequencing. The study found that gut microbiota composition was associated with overall survival (OS) and adverse events (AEs). Specifically, higher abundance of Fusicatenibacter and Butyricicoccus was linked to better OS in the NIC arm compared to the PC arm, and these genera were also associated with a lower risk of serious AEs. Conversely, higher abundance of Prevotellaceae NK3B31 was associated with higher mortality risk in the NIC arm.

### The role of gut microbiome in modulating response to immune checkpoint inhibitor therapy in cancer

- Source handle: `P0358`
- Paper ID: `9c8304850055dda9ff76ba5868edb8fbfe85773b`
- DOI: 10.21037/atm-20-6427

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** As a review article, this work does not present new primary experimental data but synthesizes existing literature. The authors systematically summarize findings from retrospective clinical studies, prospective cohorts, and preclinical murine models regarding the correlation between gut microbiota composition (diversity, specific taxa like Faecalibacterium, Bifidobacterium, Bacteroides) and ICPI outcomes. They integrate mechanistic insights from immunology (T-cell regulation, dendritic cell maturation) and metabolism (short-chain fatty acids, bile acids). The approach involves categorizing evidence into themes: response prediction, toxicity mediation (colitis, myocarditis), antibiotic effects, and therapeutic manipulation (FMT, probiotics, synthetic biotics), supported by tables listing relevant clinical trials.
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** Provides a comprehensive synthesis of the dual role of the gut microbiome in determining both the efficacy and toxicity of ICPIs in solid tumors. It integrates clinical associations with mechanistic hypotheses (immunological and metabolic) and maps the landscape of ongoing clinical trials for microbiome-based therapies.
      - **边界与限制:** As a narrative review, it does not perform a systematic meta-analysis or provide quantitative pooled estimates. The mechanistic explanations are largely derived from preclinical models or correlative human studies, so causal relationships remain to be fully validated in large-scale randomized controlled trials. The review acknowledges heterogeneity in findings across different cancer types and geographic populations.
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** Solid tumor patients receiving ICPIs (anti-PD-1/PD-L1, anti-CTLA-4)
      - **研究发现:** Loss of microbial diversity (dysbiosis) and enrichment of certain bacteria (e.g., Faecalibacterium, Akkermansia, Bifidobacterium) are associated with improved objective response rates and survival, while depletion of these taxa or enrichment of others (e.g., Bacteroides) correlates with poor response.
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** Patients treated with broad-spectrum antibiotics prior to or during ICPI therapy
      - **研究发现:** Antibiotic use is consistently associated with reduced overall survival and progression-free survival, likely due to disruption of beneficial gut microbiota; narrow-spectrum antibiotics show less clear detrimental effects.
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** Development of immune-related adverse events (irAEs), particularly colitis and myocarditis
      - **研究发现:** Specific bacterial strains influence irAE risk; e.g., Faecalibacterium enrichment is linked to higher incidence of colitis but better tumor response, while Bacteroidetes may protect against colitis. Mechanisms involve cross-reactive T-cells primed by bacterial peptides (e.g., beta-galactosidase from Bacteroides) targeting host tissues (e.g., MYH6 in heart).
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** Preclinical and early-phase clinical trials of microbiome modulation
      - **研究发现:** Interventions such as FMT, oral live biotherapeutics (e.g., EDP1503, SER-401), and synthetic biotics are being tested to restore microbiome diversity or introduce beneficial strains, showing preliminary promise in enhancing ICPI efficacy or managing refractory colitis.
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** The review addresses the challenge that only a minority of cancer patients derive durable benefit from ICPIs, and many experience severe irAEs. It seeks to explain why responses vary among patients by examining the gut microbiome as a potential predictive biomarker and modulator of both therapeutic efficacy and toxicity, aiming to identify avenues for improving patient outcomes through microbiome-targeted strategies.
    - **Research scope:** The review focuses on the role of the gut microbiome in modulating response and toxicity to immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICPIs) in cancer patients, with specific emphasis on solid tumors (e.g., melanoma, non-small cell lung cancer, renal cell carcinoma, pancreatic adenocarcinoma). It covers clinical observations, preclinical mechanisms, and emerging therapeutic strategies involving microbiome modulation.
    - **Work summary:** This article is a narrative review summarizing current evidence on how the gut microbiome influences the efficacy and safety of ICPI therapy. It discusses the association between microbial diversity/composition and treatment outcomes, outlines putative immunological and metabolic mechanisms linking gut bacteria to anti-tumor immunity and immune-related adverse events (irAEs), and highlights the impact of antibiotics and dietary factors. The review also surveys ongoing clinical trials evaluating microbiome-based interventions such as fecal microbiota transplantation (FMT) and live biotherapeutics to enhance ICPI response or mitigate toxicity.

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**
      - **Connection to review:** Establishes the motivation for investigating the gut microbiome as a modulator of ICPI outcomes. Highlights the limitations of current biomarkers (PD-L1, TMB) and the need for novel predictors.
      - **Material:** Introduction section discussing the paradigm shift in cancer treatment, the lack of universal predictive biomarkers, and the growing interest in the tumor microenvironment and gut microbiome.
      - **本节作用:** Contextual background on the clinical problem of variable ICPI response and toxicity.
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** Provides a snapshot of the translational landscape, showing how theoretical knowledge is being tested in clinical settings. Useful for discussing the feasibility and current status of microbiome-based interventions.
      - **Material:** Table 1 and Table 2 listing phase I/II trials evaluating microbial therapies (EDP1503, SER-401, MRx0518, FMT) in various solid tumors and for managing irAEs.
      - **本节作用:** Overview of ongoing clinical trials for microbiome modulation.
    - **3.**
      - **Connection to review:** Highlights the significant impact of concomitant medications (antibiotics) and other factors (diet, aging, obesity) on microbiome composition, which must be considered when interpreting microbiome-ICPI associations.
      - **Material:** Section on 'Antibiotic mediated modulation...' and mentions of dysbiosis causes in the toxicity section.
      - **本节作用:** Discussion of confounding factors affecting microbiome analysis.
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** Associations are primarily observational or from small prospective studies; causality is not established. Findings may vary by cancer type and geography. Does not provide definitive proof of mechanism.
      - **Contribution:** Summarizes clinical evidence linking specific gut microbial signatures (e.g., Faecalibacterium, Akkermansia, Bifidobacterium vs. Bacteroides) to ICPI response rates and survival outcomes in solid tumors. Details the negative impact of broad-spectrum antibiotic use on treatment efficacy.
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** Can be used to support arguments about the predictive value of the gut microbiome for ICPI efficacy. Provides specific examples of bacterial taxa to include in discussions of microbiome-response correlations. Highlights the confounding effect of antibiotics.
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** Many mechanisms are derived from murine models or correlative human data. The exact pathways are described as 'putative' and 'not fully elucidated'. Direct causal links in humans require further validation. Specific peptide-crossreactivity details are based on specific transgenic models.
      - **Contribution:** Details putative mechanisms including: 1) Modulation of T-regulatory cells and antigen-specific T-cell accumulation in the tumor microenvironment; 2) Production of metabolites (SCFAs, deoxycholate) influencing anti-tumor tolerogenesis; 3) Cross-reactive T-cell activation via bacterial peptides (e.g., beta-galactosidase mimicking MYH6) leading to irAEs like myocarditis; 4) Role of intraepithelial lymphocytes and dendritic cells in colitis pathogenesis.
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** Provides mechanistic hypotheses to explain how the microbiome modulates ICPI efficacy and toxicity. Useful for discussing the biological plausibility of microbiome-immunotherapy interactions. Illustrates the link between local gut immunity and systemic toxicity.
    - **Planning summary:** This review serves as a foundational resource for understanding the bidirectional relationship between the gut microbiome and ICPI outcomes in solid tumors. It offers detailed descriptions of specific bacterial taxa associated with response/toxicity, plausible biological mechanisms (T-cell cross-reactivity, metabolite production), and the current state of clinical translation via microbiome modulation. It is particularly useful for establishing the context of microbiome-mediated efficacy and toxicity, identifying key candidate organisms, and outlining the rationale for interventional strategies.
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - The review is a narrative synthesis, not a systematic review with strict inclusion/exclusion criteria or meta-analytic statistics. Claims should be interpreted as summaries of emerging trends rather than definitive consensus.
    - Mechanistic insights are largely based on preclinical models (mice) or correlative human studies; causal inference in humans is limited.
    - Findings on specific bacterial taxa may vary across different cancer types, geographic regions, and study populations. Generalizations should be made cautiously.
    - The term 'solid tumors' is used broadly; specific results may apply only to certain cancers (e.g., melanoma, NSCLC, RCC).
    - Author disclosures indicate potential conflicts of interest related to industry partnerships, which should be noted when evaluating the objectivity of the review's conclusions.
##### Topic handles
    - Gut microbiome composition and diversity
    - Immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICPIs) in solid tumors
    - Predictive biomarkers for ICPI response
    - Immune-related adverse events (irAEs): colitis, myocarditis
    - Mechanisms: T-cell cross-reactivity, dendritic cell maturation, SCFA/bile acid metabolism
    - Antibiotic exposure and microbiome disruption
    - Microbiome-based therapies: FMT, live biotherapeutics, synthetic biotics

### Microbial mechanisms to improve immune checkpoint blockade responsiveness

- Source handle: `P0251`
- Paper ID: `68b67449fc944c707f68e31b629b3909e59b29ea`
- DOI: 10.1016/j.neo.2022.100818

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** 采用还原论视角，从生态水平的菌群相关性分析转向分子水平的机制解析。具体路线包括：1) 梳理建立相关性的大规模测序研究和动物模型实验；2) 验证相关性并识别宿主表型（如CD8+ T细胞浸润）；3) 深入探讨具体的分子机制，分为T细胞刺激、髓系细胞刺激和分子模拟三个子主题；4) 评估基于分子机制的工程化益生菌应用前景。
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** 系统性地从分类学相关性过渡到分子机制，详细列举了多种微生物代谢物和蛋白质如何通过特定受体（GPCRs, A2AR, STING, NOD2, TLR5）和信号通路影响宿主免疫系统。
      - **边界与限制:** 所给摘要未交代具体的文献检索策略和纳入排除标准；主要依赖已发表的小鼠模型和少量临床数据，缺乏大规模前瞻性干预试验的直接证据；对于非细菌类微生物（真菌、古菌）及抑制性微生物的关注不足。
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** 小鼠模型及患者队列中的相关性研究
      - **研究发现:** 特定细菌属（如双歧杆菌、拟杆菌、阿克曼氏菌、普拉梭菌）的存在与ICB疗效正相关，但不同研究间结果存在异质性，且相关性不等于因果性。
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** 粪菌移植（FMT）和单菌定植实验
      - **研究发现:** 来自响应者的FMT可重现抗肿瘤效应，增加肿瘤内CD8+ T细胞浸润；特定菌株（如A. muciniphila, Bifidobacterium spp.）的单菌定植足以增强ICB疗效。
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** 短链脂肪酸（SCFAs）的作用
      - **研究发现:** SCFAs（如丁酸盐）对ICB的影响具有双重性：通常促进Treg和效应T细胞分化，但在某些情况下（如抗CTLA-4治疗）可能产生负面影响。
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** 肌苷（Inosine）通路
      - **研究发现:** B. pseudolongum等产生的肌苷通过结合T细胞上的A2AR受体，协同TLR9激动剂增强抗CTLA-4疗效，但单独使用不足以完全重现表型。
    - **5.**
      - **适用条件:** Akkermansia muciniphila与STING通路
      - **研究发现:** A. muciniphila产生cdAMP，激活STING信号，诱导I型干扰素产生，招募NK细胞和树突状细胞，从而增强抗肿瘤免疫。
    - **6.**
      - **适用条件:** Enterococcus sagA蛋白与NOD2通路
      - **研究发现:** 肠球菌表达的SagA蛋白降解肽聚糖产生muropeptides，激活宿主NOD2受体，促进炎症环境和CD8+ T细胞浸润。
    - **7.**
      - **适用条件:** 分子模拟机制
      - **研究发现:** E. hirae中的噬菌体尾长测量蛋白（TMP）含有与宿主PSMB4蛋白相似的表位，引发交叉反应性CD8+ T细胞，增强抗PD-1疗效。
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** 尽管已知特定微生物群与ICB疗效相关，但确切的分子机制尚不清楚，这限制了将微生物作为精准医疗手段加以利用的能力。本文试图回答：哪些具体的微生物分子特征（蛋白质、酶、代谢物）介导了这种调节作用？
    - **Research scope:** 人类微生物群（特别是肠道菌群）与癌症免疫治疗中免疫检查点阻断（ICB，如抗PD-1/PD-L1和抗CTLA-4抗体）疗效之间的关系。研究范围涵盖从宏观的菌群组成相关性到微观的分子机制（代谢物、蛋白质、抗原模拟），以及基于这些机制的工程化益生菌策略。
    - **Work summary:** 本文是一篇综述，旨在总结宿主-微生物相互作用在检查点抑制剂免疫治疗背景下的分子理解进展。文章首先回顾了通过小鼠模型和患者队列建立的菌群与ICB疗效之间的相关性，指出仅凭分类学关联难以确定因果关系。随后，重点阐述了具体的微生物分子机制，包括短链脂肪酸（SCFAs）、肌苷（inosine）、环二腺苷酸（cdAMP）、肽聚糖片段（muropeptides）和鞭毛蛋白（flagellin）如何分别通过T细胞刺激、髓系细胞激活或分子模拟来增强抗肿瘤免疫。最后，讨论了利用这些分子特征设计下一代工程化益生菌以改善治疗反应的潜力，并指出了当前研究在真菌/古菌、抑制性微生物及远端器官微生物方面的局限。

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**
      - **Connection to review:** 可用于综述的背景部分，说明当前研究的局限性及未来方向，强调从单一细菌关注扩展到多界微生物和全身微生态系统的必要性。
      - **Material:** 综述指出目前大多数研究集中在细菌，而忽略了真菌、古菌以及具有免疫抑制作用的负相关微生物；同时提到了远端器官（如肿瘤内部）微生物群的潜在作用。
      - **本节作用:** 背景介绍与领域现状
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** 可用于讨论部分，比较不同干预策略的可行性、精准度和临床转化潜力，为提出综合治疗建议提供依据。
      - **Material:** 对比了生态方法（FMT、饮食调整、定义菌群）与还原论方法（小分子、工程化益生菌）的优缺点。指出前者面临标准化和个体差异挑战，后者更具精准性但开发难度大。
      - **本节作用:** 治疗策略的比较与展望
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** 这些机制大多基于小鼠模型或体外实验，部分已在早期临床试验中探索（如MRx0518），但尚未成为标准治疗指南；不同菌株间存在显著差异，不能简单外推至所有同种细菌。
      - **Contribution:** 提供了肠道微生物群影响ICB疗效的具体生物学途径和关键分子中介。例如，明确了A. muciniphila通过cdAMP-STING轴、肠球菌通过SagA-NOD2轴、以及双歧杆菌通过肌苷-A2AR轴增强抗肿瘤免疫的具体机制。
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** 用于回答“肠道微生物群如何调节实体瘤ICB疗效”的问题，提供具体的分子层面的解释，而非仅仅停留在菌群丰度的统计关联上。
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** 机制的复杂性在于多种微生物可能通过相同或不同的路径产生相似效果（冗余性），且单一代谢物往往不足以完全重现体内复杂的生态效应（如肌苷需配合TLR9激动剂）。
      - **Contribution:** 详细描述了介导效应的具体分子机制，包括代谢物（SCFAs, inosine, cdAMP）、蛋白质（SagA, flagellin）和抗原模拟（TMP）。解释了这些分子如何被宿主受体识别并触发下游信号级联反应（如I型干扰素产生、Th1分化、CD8+ T细胞浸润）。
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** 直接支撑“什么机制介导了该效应”的子问题，提供详细的信号通路图谱和分子互作细节。
    - **Planning summary:** 该综述为解释肠道微生物群如何调节实体瘤免疫检查点阻断疗效提供了丰富的分子机制素材。它不仅列出了关键的关联物种，更重点揭示了具体的效应分子（如cdAMP, inosine, muropeptides）及其对应的宿主受体和信号通路，是构建“机制”部分的核心参考。同时，文中关于工程化益生菌的设计思路为讨论未来治疗策略提供了具体案例。
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - 文中提到的许多机制（如cdAMP, SagA）主要在小鼠模型中验证，其在人体内的确切效力和安全性仍需更多临床数据支持。
    - 作者强调“还原论”方法的优势，但这并不意味着生态方法无效，两者应被视为互补而非对立。
    - 不同菌株甚至同一物种的不同菌株之间可能存在巨大的功能差异（如Bifidobacterium bifidum的不同菌株对PD-1阻断的影响不同），因此在引用具体机制时需严格限定菌株来源。
    - SCFAs（特别是丁酸盐）对ICB的影响具有情境依赖性（如对CTLA-4可能有负面作用），不能一概而论地认为其总是有益。
##### Topic handles
    - 免疫检查点阻断（ICB）耐药性与响应性
    - 肠道微生物群组成与疗效的相关性
    - 短链脂肪酸（SCFAs）的双向免疫调节

### Facts and Hopes for Gut Microbiota Interventions in Cancer Immunotherapy

- Source handle: `P0261`
- Paper ID: `6f9060daa22a7c9efcd4a92919035c5e5c24367a`
- DOI: 10.1158/1078-0432.ccr-21-1129

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** As a narrative review, the authors systematically summarize and categorize existing literature rather than conducting a new systematic meta-analysis. The approach involves: (1) reviewing preclinical mouse models to establish causal links between specific bacteria and ICI response; (2) analyzing human observational cohort studies to identify associations between microbiota composition (taxa and diversity) and clinical outcomes (response vs. non-response); (3) synthesizing proposed biological mechanisms (T-cell activation, mimicry, metabolites); and (4) evaluating early-phase clinical trial data for microbiome interventions (FMT, consortia, diet). The review explicitly compares findings across different studies to highlight inconsistencies in biomarker discovery and emphasizes the need for standardized methodologies.
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** Provides a comprehensive synthesis of preclinical and clinical evidence linking the gut microbiome to ICI efficacy and toxicity, categorizing mechanisms into three distinct themes.
      - **边界与限制:** As a narrative review, it does not perform a quantitative meta-analysis; it highlights the lack of consensus in human biomarker studies rather than resolving it.
    - **2.**
      - **Contribution:** Critically evaluates the limitations of current human studies, including small sample sizes, geographic biases, and analytical pipeline differences, advocating for standardized large-scale datasets.
      - **边界与限制:** Does not provide definitive answers on which specific microbial signatures are universally predictive across all solid tumor types.
    - **3.**
      - **Contribution:** Summarizes early-phase clinical trial data for various microbiome interventions (FMT, consortia, diet), offering a landscape of current therapeutic developments.
      - **边界与限制:** Most clinical trial data reported are from early-phase (I/II) studies with small sample sizes; long-term efficacy and safety profiles are not yet established.
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** Preclinical mouse models
      - **研究发现:** Specific commensals (e.g., Bifidobacterium spp., Akkermansia muciniphila, Bacteroides fragilis) enhance anti-PD-1 or anti-CTLA-4 efficacy via dendritic cell activation, STING pathway signaling, and modulation of T-cell subsets.
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** Human observational studies (Melanoma, NSCLC, RCC)
      - **研究发现:** Responders to ICIs often show higher abundance of specific taxa (e.g., Faecalibacterium, Ruminococcaceae, A. muciniphila), but there is no consensus on a universal predictive signature due to cohort-specific variations, geographic differences, and methodological heterogeneity.
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** Antibiotic use during ICI therapy
      - **研究发现:** Broad-spectrum antibiotic use is consistently associated with reduced progression-free survival and overall survival in ICI-treated patients, likely due to depletion of beneficial microbes.
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** Mechanistic pathways
      - **研究发现:** Three primary mechanisms are identified: (1) direct stimulation of antitumor T-cells; (2) molecular mimicry between bacterial and tumor antigens; (3) production of immunomodulatory metabolites like short-chain fatty acids (SCFAs), secondary bile acids, and inosine.
    - **5.**
      - **适用条件:** Immune-related adverse events (irAEs)
      - **研究发现:** Specific microbiota profiles are linked to irAEs, particularly colitis. For example, certain Firmicutes are associated with anti-CTLA-4 colitis, and Streptococcus abundance correlates with arthritis and poor prognosis.
    - **6.**
      - **适用条件:** Clinical trials of FMT
      - **研究发现:** FMT from responders to PD-1 refractory melanoma patients can restore sensitivity to PD-1 inhibitors, with some patients achieving objective responses, though engraftment stability varies.
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** The review addresses the question of how the gut microbiome impacts the efficacy and toxicity of cancer immunotherapies, aiming to consolidate preclinical and clinical evidence to understand the rationale for microbiome-targeted therapies and identify gaps in current knowledge regarding predictive biomarkers and mechanistic pathways.
    - **Research scope:** The review focuses on the role of the gut microbiome in modulating responses to immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs) targeting PD-1/PD-L1 and CTLA-4 in patients with advanced solid tumors (melanoma, NSCLC, RCC, GI cancers). It covers preclinical evidence from mouse models, observational human data linking microbiota composition to clinical outcomes and adverse events, potential biological mechanisms, and early-phase clinical trials of microbiome-based interventions.
    - **Work summary:** This review synthesizes current knowledge on how the gut microbiome influences cancer immunotherapy. It begins by summarizing preclinical studies demonstrating that specific commensal bacteria enhance ICI efficacy through T-cell stimulation and metabolite production. It then reviews human observational studies, highlighting the inconsistent identification of predictive microbial signatures across different cohorts and cancer types, while noting the negative impact of broad-spectrum antibiotics. The paper outlines three main mechanistic themes: direct T-cell stimulation, molecular mimicry, and immunomodulatory metabolites (e.g., SCFAs, bile acids, inosine). It also discusses the microbiome's role in immune-related adverse events (irAEs), particularly colitis. Finally, it evaluates emerging therapeutic strategies, including fecal microbiota transplantation (FMT), defined bacterial consortia, probiotics, and dietary interventions, based on results from early-phase clinical trials.

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**
      - **Connection to review:** Contextualizes the field by highlighting the discrepancy between preclinical success and human biomarker inconsistency.
      - **Material:** Discussion of cohort-specific microbial signatures, geographic effects (enterotypes), and methodological heterogeneity (sequencing pipelines, response definitions).
      - **本节作用:** To explain why identifying a universal microbiome biomarker for ICI response remains difficult, providing context for future research directions.
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** Provides background on dietary interventions as a non-invasive way to modulate the microbiome.
      - **Material:** Details on high-fiber diets, ketogenic diets, and fasting-mimicking diets affecting microbiota composition and ICI outcomes.
      - **本节作用:** To include lifestyle/dietary factors as part of the broader strategy for microbiome modulation in cancer therapy.
    - **3.**
      - **Connection to review:** Offers a classification of current clinical trials involving microbiome therapeutics.
      - **Material:** Table 2 listing various trials (FMT, consortia, probiotics, prebiotics) with their phases, indications, and products.
      - **本节作用:** To map the current landscape of clinical development for microbiome-based adjuncts to immunotherapy.
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** The association is correlational in humans; causality is primarily established in mouse models. Predictive signatures are not consistent across all studies or cancer types.
      - **Contribution:** Provides evidence that specific gut bacteria (e.g., Bifidobacterium, Akkermansia, Faecalibacterium) are associated with improved response to PD-1/CTLA-4 inhibitors in melanoma, NSCLC, and RCC. It details how broad-spectrum antibiotics negatively impact ICI efficacy.
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** Use to support the argument that the gut microbiome modulates ICI efficacy. Can be used to list specific taxa associated with better outcomes and to cite the negative impact of antibiotics as evidence of microbiome importance.
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** Many mechanisms are derived from preclinical mouse models; human validation is ongoing. The relative contribution of each mechanism in humans is not fully quantified.
      - **Contribution:** Outlines three mechanistic categories: (1) Direct T-cell stimulation (e.g., via DC activation or TLR signaling); (2) Molecular mimicry (cross-reactive T-cells); (3) Metabolite production (SCFAs, bile acids, inosine) influencing the tumor microenvironment and systemic immunity.
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** Use to answer 'what mechanisms mediate that effect'. Provides specific biological pathways and molecules (e.g., STING pathway, NOD2, adenosine receptors, GPRs) that link microbial presence to immune function.
    - **3.**
      - **Boundaries:** Links are largely observational; causal relationships for non-colitis irAEs are less established.
      - **Contribution:** Discusses the role of the microbiome in irAEs, specifically colitis and arthritis, linking specific taxa (e.g., Firmicutes, Streptococcus) to these toxicities.
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** Use to discuss the dual role of the microbiome in both efficacy and toxicity. Highlights the complexity of microbiome modulation, as enhancing immunity may increase adverse events.
    - **4.**
      - **Boundaries:** Data are from small, early-phase trials; long-term durability and optimal donor selection criteria are not yet resolved.
      - **Contribution:** Reports early clinical trial results for FMT and defined consortia (e.g., CBM588, SER-401) showing potential to restore ICI sensitivity in refractory patients.
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** Use to illustrate the translational potential of microbiome interventions. Provides concrete examples of successful re-challenge strategies.
    - **Planning summary:** This review serves as a foundational resource for understanding the biological plausibility and clinical status of microbiome-immunotherapy interactions. It provides detailed mechanistic hypotheses (direct T-cell stimulation, mimicry, metabolites) and a critical assessment of why human biomarker discovery has been challenging (cohort heterogeneity). It also offers concrete examples of intervention strategies (FMT, consortia) and their preliminary clinical outcomes, which are essential for discussing the transition from association to causation and therapy.
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - The review covers multiple solid tumor types (melanoma, NSCLC, RCC, GI cancers); findings may not be generalizable to hematologic malignancies or other cancer types not discussed.
    - Human data are primarily observational associations; causal claims should be attributed to preclinical models unless supported by interventional trial data.
    - Biomarker findings are inconsistent across studies; do not present any single taxon as a definitive universal predictor without noting the cohort-specific nature of the evidence.
    - Clinical trial results are from early phases; avoid overstating efficacy or safety based on small sample sizes.

### Exploring the role of the gut microbiome in modulating response to anti-PD-1 immunotherapy in melanoma patients

- Source handle: `P0250`
- Paper ID: `67a11dcbb1430387e3630bb0ffa834eccc29fa4f`
- DOI: 10.18231/j.ijcbr.2024.033

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** 本文是一篇综述（Review）。作者通过梳理现有文献，系统阐述了肠道微生物组与宿主免疫系统之间的相互作用机制，重点分析了特定微生物物种、代谢产物（如短链脂肪酸）以及菌群失调对抗PD-1免疫治疗疗效的影响。文章进一步归纳了基于微生物组的干预策略，包括粪便微生物群移植（FMT）、饮食干预和益生菌使用，并讨论了其在黑色素瘤治疗中的潜力与挑战。
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** 综合了肠道微生物组影响抗PD-1疗法疗效的多种机制，包括T细胞激活、代谢物调节及免疫检查点表达调控；汇总了FMT、饮食和益生菌等干预策略的临床前及早期临床研究证据；明确了特定菌种（如Akkermansia muciniphila, Bifidobacterium）和菌群多样性作为预测生物标志物的潜在价值。
      - **边界与限制:** 作为综述，其结论依赖于所引用的原始研究，未提供新的实验数据或独立验证；对于不同干预策略的有效性和安全性比较缺乏直接的头对头证据；部分机制（如具体菌株与免疫细胞的精确分子通路）仍基于动物模型或小型临床试验，外推至所有实体瘤存在不确定性；摘要未交代具体的文献检索策略、纳入排除标准及质量评估方法。
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** 在黑色素瘤患者中，接受抗PD-1治疗且肠道菌群中含有特定有益菌（如Akkermansia muciniphila, Bifidobacterium）或具有高多样性的个体。
      - **研究发现:** 这些患者表现出更好的治疗反应和生存获益，机制涉及增强CD8+ T细胞活化、增加肿瘤浸润及促进Th1/Th17分化。
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** 肠道菌群失调（Dysbiosis），表现为有益菌减少、有害菌增多或多样性降低。
      - **研究发现:** 与抗PD-1治疗耐药相关，机制包括调节性T细胞（Tregs）扩增、髓源性抑制细胞（MDSCs）作用增强以及效应T细胞向肿瘤微环境的浸润减少。
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** 使用粪便微生物群移植（FMT）将健康供体菌群转移至对免疫治疗耐药的黑色素瘤患者。
      - **研究发现:** Davari等人（2021）的研究显示，FMT可恢复有利的免疫环境，增强T细胞活化，使部分先前耐药的患者重新获得对PD-1阻断的反应。
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** 摄入富含纤维的饮食以支持短链脂肪酸（SCFAs）产生菌的生长。
      - **研究发现:** SCFAs（如丁酸、丙酸、乙酸）可通过抑制组蛋白去乙酰化酶（HDACs）和促进抗炎信号通路，增强抗肿瘤免疫反应，改善抗PD-1疗效。
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** 尽管抗PD-1免疫疗法显著改善了黑色素瘤患者的生存率，但疗效存在高度异质性。本研究旨在探讨肠道微生物组如何调节这种疗效差异，阐明其背后的免疫学机制，并评估通过调节微生物组（如FMT、饮食、益生菌）来增强免疫治疗效果的潜在策略。
    - **Research scope:** 研究对象为黑色素瘤患者的肠道微生物组及其与抗PD-1免疫治疗的相互作用。情境涵盖从基础免疫机制（T细胞激活、代谢物作用）到临床干预策略（FMT、饮食、益生菌）的多个层面。范围主要聚焦于黑色素瘤，但也提及对其他癌症的潜在启示。
    - **Work summary:** 本综述旨在解决抗PD-1免疫疗法在黑色素瘤治疗中疗效变异性大的问题。文章首先介绍了肠道微生物组在维持免疫稳态和启动抗肿瘤免疫中的核心作用，详细阐述了微生物通过直接接触（如GALT中的抗原呈递）和间接代谢产物（如SCFAs）调节宿主免疫系统的机制。随后，文章总结了与良好治疗反应相关的微生物特征（如高多样性、特定菌属丰度）以及与耐药相关的菌群失调状态。最后，综述评估了三种主要的微生物组干预策略：FMT在逆转耐药方面的初步成功、高纤维饮食对SCFA产生的促进作用，以及益生菌使用的潜在益处与安全风险。文章指出，理解并操纵微生物组有望实现更个性化的癌症免疫治疗，但仍需更多临床研究来优化方案并确保安全性。

    - **2.**

      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** 用于回答微生物组如何被用来调节ICIs疗效的问题，特别是通过列举具体的干预手段及其预期效果。可为后续规划提供关于哪些干预策略值得深入调查的线索。

##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - 本文主要聚焦于黑色素瘤，其结论在其他实体瘤中的适用性需结合其他研究进行验证，不能直接外推。
    - 文中提到的机制（如特定菌种与T细胞活化的关系）多基于已发表的研究汇总，部分可能源自动物模型，临床因果关系尚未完全确立。
    - FMT的证据主要来自小规模研究（如Davari et al.），其长期安全性和最佳实践方案仍在探索中，不应视为成熟的标准治疗方案。
    - 益生菌的使用存在安全争议，文中明确提到需谨慎，避免将其简单推荐为通用辅助疗法。
    - 综述未提供具体的文献检索策略和质量评估细节，因此其结论的代表性取决于其所引用的原始研究的质量。

### Potential role of gut microbes in the efficacy and toxicity of immune checkpoints inhibitors

- Source handle: `P0231`
- Paper ID: `608ca9e37ce71dcbbe9281286a7b96096c05b73c`
- DOI: 10.3389/fphar.2023.1170591

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** 本研究采用文献综述的方法。作者检索并整理了近年来关于肠道微生物群与ICI疗效及毒性关系的研究，重点分析了不同癌症类型中响应者与无响应者/发生irAEs患者之间的微生物组成差异（基于16S rRNA测序和宏基因组测序数据）。文章通过表格汇总了各研究中涉及的细菌门类、属种及其与临床结局的关联，并归纳了相关的代谢机制和动物实验验证结果。最后，综述了FMT及其他微生态调节策略的临床应用现状、局限性及未来方向。
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** 系统汇总了不同实体瘤中肠道微生物群与ICI疗效及irAEs关联的现有证据，提供了按癌症类型和微生物门类分类的详细参考表（Table 1, 2, 3, 4, 5），有助于识别潜在的生物标志物。
      - **边界与限制:** 作为综述，其结论依赖于所纳入研究的原始数据质量，而原始研究多为观察性队列，存在混杂因素（如饮食、抗生素使用）和方法学差异。文章未提供新的实验数据，对因果机制的解释主要基于作者对现有文献的综合推断，缺乏大规模前瞻性验证。
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** 不同实体瘤类型（黑色素瘤、NSCLC、RCC、HCC等）
      - **研究发现:** 厚壁菌门（Firmicutes）和放线菌门（Actinobacteria）中的某些菌种（如双歧杆菌、普拉梭菌）常与ICI疗效正相关；拟杆菌门（Bacteroidetes）的作用具有情境依赖性，部分情况下与疗效负相关或与irAEs相关；变形菌门（Proteobacteria）通常与无响应或较差预后相关；粘质沙雷氏菌（Akkermansia muciniphila）在多种肿瘤中被发现与良好疗效相关。
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** 免疫相关不良事件（irAEs）的发生
      - **研究发现:** irAEs的发生与特定的微生物组成有关。例如，厚壁菌门在某些研究中与高概率的irAEs相关，而在另一些研究中（如肝细胞癌）其高丰度可能具有保护作用。拟杆菌门在某些情况下与严重的irAEs（如结肠炎）相关。变形菌门、酸杆菌门（Acidobacteria）等在irAEs患者中富集。
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** 微生物代谢产物
      - **研究发现:** 有益代谢物如短链脂肪酸（SCFAs）、多糖A（PSA）、肌苷（inosine）可通过调节T细胞功能、抑制炎症或增强抗肿瘤免疫来促进ICI疗效。有害代谢物如脂多糖（LPS）、氨、酚类物质可能促进免疫逃逸或慢性炎症。
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** 粪菌移植（FMT）
      - **研究发现:** FMT在小样本临床试验和动物模型中显示出增强ICI疗效（如增加CD8+ T细胞浸润）和缓解难治性ICI相关结肠炎的潜力，但存在感染风险、供体选择异质性和长期效果不确定性等局限。
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** 研究动机在于ICIs虽然显著改善了多种癌症患者的生存率，但响应率低（13%-69%）且伴随不可预测的免疫相关不良事件（irAEs）。现有证据表明肠道微生物群可能影响这些结果，但具体哪些微生物、通过何种机制影响疗效和毒性尚需系统总结，以便指导临床个性化治疗和开发新的干预策略。
    - **Research scope:** 免疫检查点抑制剂（ICIs）在实体瘤治疗中的应用，聚焦于肠道微生物群对疗效和免疫相关不良事件（irAEs）的影响，以及粪菌移植（FMT）等干预手段。
    - **Work summary:** 这是一篇2023年发表的综述文章，旨在探讨肠道微生物群如何调节免疫检查点抑制剂（ICIs）的疗效和毒性。文章首先介绍了肠道微生物作为“隐藏器官”在维持肠道屏障和免疫监视中的作用，随后系统梳理了不同实体瘤（如转移性黑色素瘤、非小细胞肺癌、肾细胞癌、肝细胞癌等）中，特定细菌门类和物种与ICI疗效及irAEs发生的相关性。文章还讨论了微生物代谢产物（如短链脂肪酸、肌苷等）在介导免疫反应中的潜在机制，并总结了通过粪菌移植（FMT）、益生菌、益生元等手段调节肠道菌群以增强疗效或减轻毒性的临床进展与挑战。
